Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. 18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

      "content": "---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",
      "numLines": 276,

    }

20	# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）
21	
22	> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。
23	> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）
28	
29	### 1.1 数字必须有源头
30	
31	- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件
32	- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据
33	- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报
34	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance
35	
36	> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。
37	
38	### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用
39	
40	- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol
41	- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明
42	- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较
43	- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 "at unmatched volumes"）
44	
45	> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。
46	
47	### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活
48	
49	- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文
50	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复
51	- 术语修正后不可回退（如 "same norm" → "same per-layer standard deviation"）
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配
56	
57	### 2.1 Claim 强度规则
58	
59	| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |
60	|----------|--------------|
61	| 单 seed, 单数据集 | "preliminary observation", "initial evidence" |
62	| 单 seed, 多数据集 | "suggestive pattern", "indicative trend" |
63	| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 "we find" |
64	| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 "robust", "consistent" |
65	| 统计检验显著 | 可用 "significant", 附 p-value |
66	| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 "scale-invariant" 等 |
67	
68	### 2.2 禁止的过强表述
69	
70	- "remarkably stable" → 改为 "relatively stable" 或给出量化波动范围
71	- "confirmed" → 仅 "suggestive" 级别数据不能用
72	- "scale-invariant" → single-seed 验证不够
73	- "We attribute X to Y" → 如果是假说，必须改为 "We hypothesize" + "requiring causal verification"
74	- "roughly twice" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似
75	- "domain-agnostic" → 改为 "robust across N domains tested"
76	
77	### 2.3 Claim 降级机制
78	
79	当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：
80	- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落
81	- 硬 claim → 加 caveat 的软 claim
82	- 正文数字 → 移入 Appendix
83	
84	---
85	
86	## 三、叙事与 Storytelling
87	
88	### 3.1 结构原则
89	
90	- 核心 paradox/发现之间必须有**过渡句**桥接，不能让读者在关键处断线
91	- Introduction 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
92	- Discussion 不应重复 Results 的具体数字——引用表格编号
93	- 同一解释不要在多处重复推导——主文写一次 + 指向 Appendix
94	- Limitations 段不要写成一个 17 行大段——拆分为 2-3 个子段按主题分组
95	
96	### 3.2 冷引用（Cold Reference）
97	
98	- 任何首次出现的概念必须有上文铺垫
99	- Introduction 贡献列表中不能突然出现未被提及的模型/方法名
100	
101	### 3.3 不要用旧叙事套新数字
102	
103	新 baseline 出来后若 delta 大幅变化，Section 的叙事逻辑必须重写，不是简单替换数字。
104	
105	### 3.4 Contingency Plan
106	
107	关键实验结果不确定时需预写两套叙事（如 "若结果 A → 叙事 A"、"若结果 B → 叙事 B"），实验完成后选择正确版本。提交前必须确认只留了一套。
108	
109	### 3.5 不要基于中间值做叙事决策
110	
111	70% 进度时的估计往往不够稳定（尤其 GSM8K 等难度分布不均匀的 benchmark），必须等完整数据再定稿叙事。
112	
113	---
114	
115	## 四、术语与一致性
116	
117	### 4.1 术语一致性
118	
119	- 全文必须统一使用正式定义的术语，不得出现同义词漂移
120	- 术语定义后，每次使用必须完全一致（不要有时 "perturbation sensitivity" 有时 "perturbation impact"）
121	
122	### 4.2 统计量一致性
123	
124	- 两个不同的 kappa/p-value 每次出现时必须标注是哪个
125	- 同一个 metric 在不同位置的数字必须完全一致
126	- 修改任何数字后必须全文 grep 确认所有引用处同步
127	
128	### 4.3 p-value 格式
129	
130	- 无前导零、3 位小数：`p = .003`（不是 `p = 0.003`）
131	- 当非劣效性 p<0.001 实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明
132	
133	### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致
134	
135	Taxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 "no new content"，示例中就不能包含新信息。
136	
137	---
138	
139	## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）
140	
141	### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）
142	
143	提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：
144	```
145	XX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \placeholder{
146	```
147	所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。
148	
149	### 5.2 编译检查
150	
151	- [ ] 编译零 error
152	- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用
153	- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox
154	- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）
155	- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）
156	- [ ] Review 版启用行号
157	- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢
158	
159	### 5.3 数据完整性
160	
161	- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过
162	- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认
163	- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明
164	- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差
165	- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明
166	
167	### 5.4 图表检查
168	
169	- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配
170	- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）
171	- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt
172	- [ ] 小数精度全表统一
173	- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线
174	- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含
175	- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）
176	
177	### 5.5 引用检查
178	
179	- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）
180	- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级
181	- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配
182	- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目
183	- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）
184	- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确
185	- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐
186	
187	### 5.6 匿名化检查
188	
189	- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）
190	- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径
191	- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）
192	- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）
193	- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露
194	
195	### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）
196	
197	- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围
198	- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充
199	- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）
200	- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用
201	- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间
202	- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours
203	- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整
204	- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况
205	- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）
206	
207	### 5.8 叙事完整性
208	
209	- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 "under greedy decoding"）
210	- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 "single seed" 另一个已是 "10 seeds"）
211	- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留
212	- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆
213	- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）
214	- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）
215	- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）
216	
217	### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查
218	
219	- [ ] **`grep "—" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）
220	- [ ] **`grep " – " paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）
221	- [ ] 无 "-ing," 分词短语结尾（如 "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."）
222	- [ ] 无连续 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"（同段最多 1 个）
223	- [ ] 无 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."
224	- [ ] 无 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "a myriad of" / "a plethora of"
225	- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段
226	
227	### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对
228	
229	- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）
230	- [ ] `\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段
231	- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐
232	
233	---
234	
235	## 六、Reviewer 审查标准
236	
237	### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）
238	
239	- 论文数字与实验数据不一致
240	- Abstract 与正文矛盾
241	- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）
242	- 未经验证的数据写入论文
243	- 占位符残留
244	
245	### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）
246	
247	- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）
248	- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑
249	- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 "3×3×3=27" 但实际只有 15 格）
250	- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain
251	- 旧版数据引用
252	
253	### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程
254	
255	1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics
256	2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）
257	3. 独立重算所有衍生值
258	4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL
259	5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内
260	
261	---
262	
263	## 七、常见反模式速查表
264	
265	| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |
266	|--------|------|----------|
267	| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |
268	| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |
269	| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |
270	| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |
271	| 叙事超越数据 | "confirmed" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |
272	| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |
273	| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |
274	| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |
275	| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |
276	| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |
277	| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |
278	| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |
279	| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |
280	| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |
281	
282	---
283	
284	## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills
285	
286	写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：
287	- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式
288	- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计
289	- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范
290	- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训
291	
292	---
293	
294	## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训
295	
296	1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改
297	2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备
298	3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理
299	4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证
300	5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿
301	
````

</details>

实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明\n\n### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致\n\nTaxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 \"no new content\"，示例中就不能包含新信息。\n\n---\n\n## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）\n\n### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）\n\n提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：\n```\nXX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \\placeholder{\n```\n所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。\n\n### 5.2 编译检查\n\n- [ ] 编译零 error\n- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用\n- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox\n- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）\n- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）\n- [ ] Review 版启用行号\n- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢\n\n### 5.3 数据完整性\n\n- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过\n- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认\n- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明\n- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差\n- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明\n\n### 5.4 图表检查\n\n- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配\n- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）\n- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt\n- [ ] 小数精度全表统一\n- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线\n- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含\n- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）\n\n### 5.5 引用检查\n\n- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）\n- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级\n- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配\n- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目\n- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）\n- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确\n- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐\n\n### 5.6 匿名化检查\n\n- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）\n- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径\n- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）\n- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）\n- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露\n\n### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）\n\n- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围\n- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充\n- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）\n- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用\n- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间\n- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours\n- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整\n- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况\n- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）\n\n### 5.8 叙事完整性\n\n- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 \"under greedy decoding\"）\n- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 \"single seed\" 另一个已是 \"10 seeds\"）\n- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留\n- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆\n- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）\n- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）\n- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）\n\n### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查\n\n- [ ] **`grep \"—\" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n- [ ] **`grep \" – \" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）\n- [ ] 无 \"-ing,\" 分词短语结尾（如 \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"）\n- [ ] 无连续 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"（同段最多 1 个）\n- [ ] 无 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"\n- [ ] 无 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\"\n- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段\n\n### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对\n\n- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）\n- [ ] `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段\n- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐\n\n---\n\n## 六、Reviewer 审查标准\n\n### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）\n\n- 论文数字与实验数据不一致\n- Abstract 与正文矛盾\n- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）\n- 未经验证的数据写入论文\n- 占位符残留\n\n### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）\n\n- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）\n- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑\n- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 \"3×3×3=27\" 但实际只有 15 格）\n- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain\n- 旧版数据引用\n\n### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程\n\n1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics\n2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）\n3. 独立重算所有衍生值\n4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL\n5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内\n\n---\n\n## 七、常见反模式速查表\n\n| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |\n|--------|------|----------|\n| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |\n| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |\n| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |\n| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |\n| 叙事超越数据 | \"confirmed\" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |\n| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |\n| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |\n| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |\n| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |\n| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |\n| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |\n| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |\n| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |\n| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |\n\n---\n\n## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills\n\n写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：\n- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式\n- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计\n- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范\n- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训\n\n---\n\n## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训\n\n1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改\n2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备\n3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理\n4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证\n5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿\n",

    }

```text
1	% Mirror of abstract in main.tex — do not edit here.
2	
3	Multi-judge panels are widely used to defend Large Language Model (LLM) evaluation against manipulation, yet no existing work disentangles whether robustness gains stem from individual judge quality or inter-judge diversity. We decompose panel attack success rate into weakest-link susceptibility ($\eta_{\max}$) and effective independence ($K_{\mathrm{eff}}$) across 15 models, six attack types, two multiple-choice benchmarks, and panel sizes $K \in \{3, 5, 7\}$ (up to 6,435 panels). Two findings are robust across diversity metrics and inference methods: (1)~diversity is uniformly positively associated with vulnerability (both $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\kappa$-diversity confirm positive direction in 12/12 conditions), and (2)~$\eta_{\max}$'s predictive power diminishes monotonically with panel size (meta-analytic pooled $\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}$: $0.264 \to 0.102 \to 0.084$; confidence interval crosses zero at $K{\geq}5$). Monte Carlo calibration confirms the wild cluster bootstrap is conservative (max null rejection $1.6\%$ at $\alpha{=}5\%$). Aggregation rules modulate predictability ($R^2$: threshold 0.83, Bayesian 0.09). The diversity--capability confound ($r{=}0.70$--$0.81$) precludes causal claims; all findings are associational.
4	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	\begin{figure*}[t]
4	\centering
5	\includegraphics[width=0.78\textwidth]{figures/fig_framework.pdf}
6	\caption{The diminishing weakest link in LLM judge panels. \textbf{(a)}~At $K{=}3$, one compromised judge flips the majority; $\eta_{\max}$ dominates ($\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}{=}0.264$). \textbf{(b)}~Predictor shift: $\eta_{\max}$'s effect declines monotonically while $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ rises; the transition zone $K^* \in (3,5)$ marks the crossover. \textbf{(c)}~At $K{\geq}5$, the adversary must compromise multiple judges; $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ dominates ($\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}{=}0.436$) while $\eta_{\max}$'s CI crosses zero ($\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}{=}0.084$).}
7	\label{fig:framework}
8	\end{figure*}
9	
10	Multi-judge panels have become the dominant defense against bias and manipulation in Large Language Model (LLM) evaluation. After \citet{zheng2023judging} demonstrated that individual LLM judges can rival human annotators, subsequent work exposed systematic vulnerabilities: position bias, verbosity preferences, and sycophancy compromise the reliability of any single judge \citep{wang2023fair}. The natural response has been to aggregate decisions across diverse models, an approach inspired by Byzantine fault tolerance (BFT), where majority voting among independent nodes tolerates faulty responses \citep{lamport1982byzantine,castro1999practical}. A recent systematization reports that 7-model committees reduce attack success rates (ASR) from 50--68\% to 10--19\% \citep{almasoud2026security}. Diversity, by this logic, is the key ingredient for secure evaluation, yet this framing conflates two distinct sources of robustness: the quality of individual judges and the independence among them.
11	
12	The conflation matters because the BFT analogy breaks down in the LLM judge setting. BFT adversaries control faulty nodes from within; evaluation adversaries manipulate inputs from without, and shared pretraining corpora create correlated failure modes that violate the independence assumption underlying majority-vote guarantees \citep{schneier2000secrets}. No existing work disentangles diversity from individual robustness across panel sizes, so the relative contribution of each factor to observed ASR reductions remains unclear.
13	
14	We address this gap by decomposing panel vulnerability into two predictors: weakest-link susceptibility ($\eta_{\max}$, the maximum individual attack success rate in a panel) and effective independent channels ($K_{\mathrm{eff}}$, an information-theoretic measure of judge independence). Controlled experiments across 15 models (9 API, 6 local quantized), $K \in \{3,5,7\}$, six attack types, and two multiple-choice benchmarks---Massive Multitask Language Understanding (MMLU) and the AI2 Reasoning Challenge (ARC)---reveal two robust patterns and one metric-sensitive observation.
15	
16	These findings yield three contributions:
17	\begin{enumerate}
18	\item \textbf{Diversity uniformly positively associated with vulnerability.} Both $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\kappa$-diversity \citep{kuncheva2003measures} confirm a positive association with panel attack success in all 12 conditions (12/12 direction under OLS and generalized additive model bootstrap). However, the diversity--capability confound ($r{=}0.70$--$0.81$) precludes causal interpretation: Cinelli--Hazlett robustness values do not exceed the confound in 69\% of conditions (\S\ref{sec:dominance}, \S\ref{sec:gam_taxonomy}).
19	\item \textbf{Diminishing weakest link.} $\eta_{\max}$'s predictive power declines monotonically with panel size: meta-analytic pooled $\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}$ falls from $0.264$ [$0.158, 0.369$] at $K{=}3$ to $0.102$ [$-0.055, 0.259$] at $K{=}5$ to $0.084$ [$-0.083, 0.251$] at $K{=}7$; the confidence interval crosses zero at $K{\geq}5$. The diversity significance trajectory, however, depends on the metric: $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance increases ($4 \to 7 \to 8$/12) while $\kappa$-diversity decreases ($8 \to 7 \to 6$/12), indicating that the $K$-dependent pattern is operationalization-sensitive (\S\ref{sec:cross_k}).
20	\item \textbf{Aggregation as predictability control.} Aggregation rules modulate how much variance panel composition explains ($R^2$: threshold 0.83, Bayesian 0.09). Bayesian calibration attenuates $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (5+/1$-$) but $\eta_{\max}$ persists (9/12), so the defense is partial (\S\ref{sec:aggregation}, \S\ref{sec:analysis}).
21	\end{enumerate}
22	
23	All findings are associational; the diversity--capability confound ($r{=}0.70$--$0.81$; \S\ref{sec:analysis}) precludes causal claims.
24	
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{LLM-as-Judge and Multi-Judge Evaluation.}
4	LLM judges gained traction after \citet{zheng2023judging} established them as scalable alternatives to human annotation. \citet{wang2023fair} documented systematic failure modes---position bias, verbosity, sycophancy---motivating multi-judge panels, while \citet{raina2024judgerobust} and \citet{maloyan2025investigating} quantified adversarial vulnerabilities including prompt injection. Multi-judge aggregation extends the classical noisy annotator framework \citep{dawid1979maximum}: \citet{verga2024replacing} showed diverse panels can match single large judges, and \citet{panickssery2024llm} identified self-preference bias driving within-family correlations. Most relevant, \citet{almasoud2026security} reported that 7-model committees reduce ASR from 50--68\% to 10--19\% but did not disentangle diversity from individual robustness---the gap we address. Concurrent work benchmarks judge quality \citep{tan2024judgebench,li2024autoj}, adversarial risks in multi-agent systems \citep{tamas2025benchmarking}, and collaborative evaluation under benign conditions \citep{qian2026collabeval}; none tests whether aggregation benefits survive adversarial manipulation.
5	
6	\paragraph{Condorcet Jury Theorem.}
7	\citet{condorcet1785essai} proved that majority voting accuracy converges to 1 when per-voter accuracy exceeds 0.5; \citet{ladha1992condorcet} extended the theorem to correlated voters, showing that positive correlation weakens the convergence guarantee. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) explicitly assumes independent, non-adversarial errors; its failure under adaptive adversaries is trivially expected \citep{tramer2018ensemble,kariyappa2019improving,pang2019improving}. We focus on the \emph{panel-size-dependent structure} of this vulnerability: the diminishing predictive power of the weakest link as $K$ grows, and the persistent positive association between diversity and vulnerability.
8	
9	\paragraph{Ensemble Diversity Theory.}
10	Classical ensemble theory shows diverse ensembles reduce error \citep{dietterich2000ensemble,breiman2001random,krogh1995neural}, but only when members maintain sufficiently low bias \citep{brown2005managing}; selective ensembles can outperform using all members \citep{zhou2002ensembling}. \citet{kuncheva2003measures} found no pairwise diversity measure reliably predicted improvement; our $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ exhibits a $K$-dependent pattern whose direction depends on the metric. LLM panels differ from classical ensembles: members are heterogeneous pre-trained models, and adversaries exploit natural-language manipulation rather than random noise. Recent frameworks \citep{yang2026diversity,turkmen2026ensemble} characterize diversity under benign evaluation; we provide the adversarial complement.
11	
12	\paragraph{Adversarial Robustness and Transferability.}
13	Adversarial vulnerability was formalized by \citet{goodfellow2015adversarial}, with transferability demonstrated by \citet{papernot2016transferability,papernot2017practical}. Controlled diversity via uncorrelated gradients \citep{kariyappa2019improving}, diversity regularization \citep{pang2019improving}, or vulnerability diversification \citep{zhu2020dverge} strengthens ensemble robustness in vision but requires coordinating member training---a control absent in post-hoc LLM judge selection. Our setting differs: adversaries use natural-language manipulation rather than gradient perturbations, and aggregation rules are a controllable design parameter.
14	
15	\paragraph{BFT and Security Games.}
16	Classical BFT \citep{lamport1982byzantine,castro1999practical,blanchard2017byzantine} tolerates faulty nodes but assumes non-strategic faults; \citet{schneier2000secrets} articulated the weakest-link principle. Our framework adopts the Stackelberg structure of \citet{tambe2011security}. Correlated LLM judge errors \citep{kim2025correlated,kuai2026independent} violate BFT's independence assumption; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose the Difficulty-Weighted Behavioral Entanglement Index (DBEI), weighting agreement by item difficulty, while $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ captures a complementary dimension via pairwise MI. Mitigation strategies include DBEI-based reweighting, Bayesian calibration, and credibility scoring \citep{ebrahimi2025adversary}.
17	
18	\paragraph{Information-Theoretic Evaluation.}
19	Information-theoretic approaches address statistical validity \citep{robertson2025stepwise,feng2026noisy}, attack costs \citep{kaneko2025bits}, sequential selection \citep{jung2026logarithmic}, and truthfulness \citep{chen2025incentivizing,qiu2026truthfulness}, but assume honest judges. We address adversarial manipulation interacting with multi-judge composition.
20	
```

```text
1	\section{Threat Model and Problem Formulation}
2	\label{sec:threat_model}
3	
4	A \emph{panel} $\mathcal{P} = \{J_1, \ldots, J_K\}$ consists of $K$ judges (with $K$ odd) that evaluate a candidate text via majority vote: the text is accepted if at least $t = \lceil K/2 \rceil$ judges approve it. Under an attack $a$, each judge $J_j$ is compromised with marginal failure probability $\eta_j(a) = \Pr(X_j^a = 1)$, where $X_j^a \in \{0,1\}$ denotes the failure indicator. We write $\eta_{\max} = \max_j \eta_j$ for the vulnerability of the weakest judge. The \emph{panel attack success rate} is $\mathrm{ASR}(\mathcal{P}, a) = \Pr\!\bigl(\sum_{j=1}^K X_j^a \geq t\bigr)$.
5	
6	To quantify diversity, we define the \emph{effective independence} $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ directly from pairwise mutual information (MI) among judges:
7	\begin{equation}
8	\label{eq:keff}
9	K_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{1}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K} \Bigl[1 + \sum_{j \neq i} \exp\!\bigl(-2 \cdot \mathrm{MI}(i,j)\bigr)\Bigr].
10	\end{equation}
11	This formulation computes effective independence from pairwise MI estimates without matrix decomposition, and is more stable for small panels ($K{=}3$). It ranges from $K_{\mathrm{eff}} \approx K$ when judges are nearly independent ($\mathrm{MI} \to 0$) to $K_{\mathrm{eff}} \approx 1$ when judges are highly correlated ($\mathrm{MI} \to \infty$). Motivated by information-theoretic redundancy reduction, one can informally expect joint vulnerability to scale as $\eta_{\mathrm{joint}} \sim K_{\mathrm{eff}} \cdot \eta_{\max}$: independent channels multiply failure probabilities, so fewer effective channels (lower $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$) should reduce panel vulnerability. This motivating relationship assumes non-adaptive faults and independent channels; it serves as intuition rather than a formal bound.
12	
13	\begin{definition}[Adaptive Adversary]
14	\label{def:adversary}
15	A \emph{full-knowledge (oracle) adaptive adversary} has complete knowledge of the panel composition $\mathcal{P}$, the per-judge vulnerability profiles $\{\eta_j(\cdot)\}_{j=1}^K$, and the inter-judge correlation structure. It selects the attack $a^* = \arg\max_{a \in \mathcal{A}} \mathrm{ASR}(\mathcal{P}, a)$ to maximize the panel ASR, yielding $\mathrm{ASR}^*(\mathcal{P}) = \sup_{a \in \mathcal{A}} \mathrm{ASR}(\mathcal{P}, a)$. This oracle model represents an \emph{upper bound} on adversarial capability; realistic adversaries with partial knowledge of panel composition would achieve lower ASR.
16	\end{definition}
17	
18	This departs from BFT models~\cite{lamport1982byzantine,castro1999practical}, which tolerate $f < n/3$ faulty nodes but do not model adversaries that strategically target system structure. An adaptive adversary concentrates on the weakest link~\cite{schneier2000secrets}, suggesting a \emph{reversal}: under random faults, higher $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ reduces joint failure probability, but under adaptive adversaries, diverse judges may present a wider attack surface.
19	
20	\begin{proposition}[Weakest-Link Monotonicity; formal statement and proof in Appendix~\ref{app:proof}]
21	\label{thm:monotonicity}
22	Replacing any panel member with a pointwise more vulnerable judge can only increase $\mathrm{ASR}^*(\mathcal{P})$, for any adversary including adaptive ones. The stochastic dominance condition holds for 58\% of judge pairs (61/105); non-dominance concentrates among within-tier pairs (72.1\% non-dominant, 31/43) versus cross-tier pairs (21.0\%, 13/62), reflecting that capability-similar judges lack uniform ordering.
23	\end{proposition}
24	
25	The 58\% coverage means Proposition~\ref{thm:monotonicity} applies to cross-tier substitutions (where capability gaps are large enough for uniform dominance) but not to within-tier swaps, where judges exhibit comparable vulnerability profiles with crossing points across items. Consequently, the proposition constrains panel design when selecting across capability tiers but does not predict the effect of substitutions within a tier.
26	
27	The proposition predicts $\beta_2 > 0$ in Equation~\ref{eq:regression}. The independence-based intuition suggests $\beta_1 \leq 0$ under non-adaptive faults; the reversal pattern predicts positive $\beta_1$ for judge-specific attacks and negative for shared-bias attacks. The relative importance of $\eta_{\max}$ versus $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ depends on $K$: at small $K$, one weak judge flips the majority; at larger $K$, the adversary must compromise multiple judges. We test these predictions across $K \in \{3,5,7\}$ (\S\ref{sec:experiments}).

      "content": "\\section{Threat Model and Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:threat_model}\n\nA \\emph{panel} $\\mathcal{P} = \\{J_1, \\ldots, J_K\\}$ consists of $K$ judges (with $K$ odd) that evaluate a candidate text via majority vote: the text is accepted if at least $t = \\lceil K/2 \\rceil$ judges approve it. Under an attack $a$, each judge $J_j$ is compromised with marginal failure probability $\\eta_j(a) = \\Pr(X_j^a = 1)$, where $X_j^a \\in \\{0,1\\}$ denotes the failure indicator. We write $\\eta_{\\max} = \\max_j \\eta_j$ for the vulnerability of the weakest judge. The \\emph{panel attack success rate} is $\\mathrm{ASR}(\\mathcal{P}, a) = \\Pr\\!\\bigl(\\sum_{j=1}^K X_j^a \\geq t\\bigr)$.\n\nTo quantify diversity, we define the \\emph{effective independence} $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ directly from pairwise mutual information (MI) among judges:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:keff}\nK_{\\mathrm{eff}} = \\frac{1}{K}\\sum_{i=1}^{K} \\Bigl[1 + \\sum_{j \\neq i} \\exp\\!\\bigl(-2 \\cdot \\mathrm{MI}(i,j)\\bigr)\\Bigr].\n\\end{equation}\nThis formulation computes effective independence from pairwise MI estimates without matrix decomposition, and is more stable for small panels ($K{=}3$). It ranges from $K_{\\mathrm{eff}} \\approx K$ when judges are nearly independent ($\\mathrm{MI} \\to 0$) to $K_{\\mathrm{eff}} \\approx 1$ when judges are highly correlated ($\\mathrm{MI} \\to \\infty$). Motivated by information-theoretic redundancy reduction, one can informally expect joint vulnerability to scale as $\\eta_{\\mathrm{joint}} \\sim K_{\\mathrm{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max}$: independent channels multiply failure probabilities, so fewer effective channels (lower $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$) should reduce panel vulnerability. This motivating relationship assumes non-adaptive faults and independent channels; it serves as intuition rather than a formal bound.\n\n\\begin{definition}[Adaptive Adversary]\n\\label{def:adversary}\nA \\emph{full-knowledge (oracle) adaptive adversary} has complete knowledge of the panel composition $\\mathcal{P}$, the per-judge vulnerability profiles $\\{\\eta_j(\\cdot)\\}_{j=1}^K$, and the inter-judge correlation structure. It selects the attack $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a \\in \\mathcal{A}} \\mathrm{ASR}(\\mathcal{P}, a)$ to maximize the panel ASR, yielding $\\mathrm{ASR}^*(\\mathcal{P}) = \\sup_{a \\in \\mathcal{A}} \\mathrm{ASR}(\\mathcal{P}, a)$. This oracle model represents an \\emph{upper bound} on adversarial capability; realistic adversaries with partial knowledge of panel composition would achieve lower ASR.\n\\end{definition}\n\nThis departs from BFT models~\\cite{lamport1982byzantine,castro1999practical}, which tolerate $f < n/3$ faulty nodes but do not model adversaries that strategically target system structure. An adaptive adversary concentrates on the weakest link~\\cite{schneier2000secrets}, suggesting a \\emph{reversal}: under random faults, higher $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ reduces joint failure probability, but under adaptive adversaries, diverse judges may present a wider attack surface.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Weakest-Link Monotonicity; formal statement and proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:proof}]\n\\label{thm:monotonicity}\nReplacing any panel member with a pointwise more vulnerable judge can only increase $\\mathrm{ASR}^*(\\mathcal{P})$, for any adversary including adaptive ones. The stochastic dominance condition holds for 58\\% of judge pairs (61/105); non-dominance concentrates among within-tier pairs (72.1\\% non-dominant, 31/43) versus cross-tier pairs (21.0\\%, 13/62), reflecting that capability-similar judges lack uniform ordering.\n\\end{proposition}\n\nThe 58\\% coverage means Proposition~\\ref{thm:monotonicity} applies to cross-tier substitutions (where capability gaps are large enough for uniform dominance) but not to within-tier swaps, where judges exhibit comparable vulnerability profiles with crossing points across items. Consequently, the proposition constrains panel design when selecting across capability tiers but does not predict the effect of substitutions within a tier.\n\nThe proposition predicts $\\beta_2 > 0$ in Equation~\\ref{eq:regression}. The independence-based intuition suggests $\\beta_1 \\leq 0$ under non-adaptive faults; the reversal pattern predicts positive $\\beta_1$ for judge-specific attacks and negative for shared-bias attacks. The relative importance of $\\eta_{\\max}$ versus $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ depends on $K$: at small $K$, one weak judge flips the majority; at larger $K$, the adversary must compromise multiple judges. We test these predictions across $K \\in \\{3,5,7\\}$ (\\S\\ref{sec:experiments}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lllccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Family} & \\textbf{Tier} & \\textbf{Deploy} & \\textbf{MMLU} & \\textbf{ARC} \\\\\n\\midrule\ngpt-5.5              & OpenAI    & frontier    & API   & .957 & .983 \\\\\ngemini-3.1-pro-preview & Google  & frontier    & API   & .955 & .987 \\\\\nclaude-opus-4-6       & Anthropic & frontier   & API   & .890 & .987 \\\\\ngemini-3.5-flash      & Google    & frontier   & API   & .968 & 1.00$^\\dagger$ \\\\\n\\addlinespace\ngpt-4.1               & OpenAI    & mid-tier   & API   & .837 & .967 \\\\\nclaude-sonnet-4-6     & Anthropic & mid-tier   & API   & .810 & .953 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\ngpt-4.1-mini          & OpenAI    & lightweight & API  & .800 & .963 \\\\\ngpt-4.1-nano          & OpenAI    & lightweight & API  & .633 & .853 \\\\\nclaude-3-haiku        & Anthropic & lightweight & API  & .767 & .450 \\\\\nmistral-large (123B)  & Mistral   & lightweight & local & .537 & .533 \\\\\nllama3.1-70b          & Llama     & lightweight & local & .523 & .520 \\\\\nllama3.1-8b           & Llama     & lightweight & local & .543 & .320 \\\\\nqwen2.5-72b           & Qwen      & lightweight & local & .333 & .410 \\\\\nqwen2.5-32b           & Qwen      & lightweight & local & .373 & .450 \\\\\nqwen2.5-14b           & Qwen      & lightweight & local & .253 & .240 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Judge models used in the study (clean accuracy on 300-item samples). ```text
1	\section{Experimental Methodology}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Models and Panel Construction}
5	
6	We select 15 LLM judges spanning 6 provider families and 3 capability tiers, listed in Table~\ref{tab:models}. Nine models are accessed via the OpenRouter API;\footnote{All API models were accessed via OpenRouter between March--April 2026. Model snapshots may have changed after this period.} six open-weight models are served locally using vLLM~0.21.0 with Activation-aware Weight Quantization (AWQ) INT4 on an NVIDIA RTX PRO~6000 Blackwell (96\,GB). API models are assigned to \emph{frontier}, \emph{mid-tier}, or \emph{lightweight} tiers following the provider capability classification; all six local models fall into the lightweight tier, as INT4 quantization substantially reduces judge accuracy on the pairwise judgment task (Table~\ref{tab:models}). We study three panel sizes $K \in \{3, 5, 7\}$ (all odd, majority-vote threshold $t = \lceil K/2 \rceil$). Exhaustive enumeration yields $\binom{15}{3} = 455$ panels at $K{=}3$, $\binom{15}{5} = 3{,}003$ at $K{=}5$, and $\binom{15}{7} = 6{,}435$ at $K{=}7$, covering every combination of families and tiers. Each panel evaluates all benchmark items under all attack types, producing a complete panel $\times$ item $\times$ attack tensor. The primary analysis uses $K{=}3$; \S\ref{sec:cross_k} extends to larger panels.
7	
8	\subsection{Benchmarks and Attack Types}
9	
10	Two benchmarks test distinct evaluation capabilities: MMLU \citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}, a knowledge-intensive multiple-choice benchmark spanning 57 subjects, and ARC \citep{clark2018arc}, a reasoning-intensive science question benchmark. We sample 300 items from each. All models use temperature~0 for deterministic outputs (temperature has negligible effect on $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$; Appendix~\ref{app:temperature}).
11	
12	Each benchmark is paired with six attack types: (1)~\textbf{prompt injection}, adversarial instructions overriding the evaluation prompt \citep{perez2022ignore,greshake2023not}; (2)~\textbf{sycophancy}, flattering language exploiting judge preferences \citep{wang2023fair}; (3)~\textbf{score manipulation}, explicit numerical cues anchoring scoring; (4)~\textbf{verbosity bias}, padding with irrelevant detail \citep{zheng2023judging}; (5)~\textbf{position bias}, reordering to exploit positional preferences \citep{wang2023fair}; and (6)~\textbf{authority bias}, fabricated credentials inflating credibility. The 2 benchmarks $\times$ 6 attacks produce 12 conditions, each evaluated across all 455 panels (templates and implementation details in Appendix~\ref{app:attacks}).
13	
14	\begin{table}[t]
15	\centering
16	\small
17	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
18	\toprule
19	\textbf{Bench.} & \textbf{Attack} & $\boldsymbol{\eta_{\max}}$ & $\boldsymbol{K_{\mathrm{eff}}}$ & \textbf{Dom.} \\
20	\midrule
21	MMLU & Prompt inj.    & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
22	MMLU & Sycophancy     & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
23	MMLU & Score manip.   & n.s.    & $+^{*}$ & $K$ \\
24	MMLU & Verbosity      & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
25	MMLU & Position bias  & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
26	MMLU & Authority bias & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
27	\addlinespace
28	ARC  & Prompt inj.    & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
29	ARC  & Sycophancy     & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
30	ARC  & Score manip.   & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
31	ARC  & Verbosity      & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
32	ARC  & Position bias  & n.s.    & $+^{*}$ & $K$ \\
33	ARC  & Authority bias & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\
34	\bottomrule
35	\end{tabular}
36	\caption{Standardized regression coefficients for $\eta_{\max}$ and $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ across 12 conditions (455 panels, log-log OLS). $+^{*}$/$-^{*}$: FDR-significant positive/negative (Benjamini--Hochberg, $q < 0.05$); n.s.: not significant. Dom.\ column: $\eta$ = $\eta_{\max}$ sole dominant, $K$ = $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ sole dominant, Co = co-dominant \citep{budescu1993dominance}.}
37	\label{tab:dominance}
38	\end{table}
39	
40	\subsection{Vulnerability Metrics}
41	
42	Per-judge vulnerability under attack $a$ is the fraction of items where judge $j$ is fooled: $\eta_j(a) = \Pr(X_j^a {=} 1)$, estimated empirically over the item set. The weakest-link vulnerability $\eta_{\max}(a) = \max_{j \in \mathcal{P}} \eta_j(a)$ captures the most susceptible member of each panel. The panel attack success rate $\mathrm{ASR}(\mathcal{P}, a)$ is the fraction of items where at least $t$ judges are simultaneously fooled. Panel diversity is quantified by $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (Equation~\ref{eq:keff}), computed from pairwise MI between judges' three-category winner labels (model~A wins, model~B wins, tie) on clean data, using the plug-in estimator with Miller--Madow bias correction. The $\exp(-2\,\mathrm{MI}(i,j))$ term measures pairwise alienation: 1 when independent, 0 under full dependence. Under a Gaussian copula this yields $K_{\mathrm{eff}} = K - \frac{1}{K}\sum_{i}\sum_{j \neq i} \rho_{ij}^2$ \citep{nelsen2006copulas}; goodness-of-fit rejects the Gaussian copula for 97--100\% of pairs, yet plug-in MI and closed-form rankings agree closely ($\rho{\approx}0.86$; Appendix~\ref{app:copula_gof}). Together, $\eta_{\max}$ (weakest-link channel) and $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (diversity channel) serve as the two predictors in the regression framework below.
43	
44	\subsection{Aggregation Methods}
45	
46	Beyond majority vote ($\geq \lceil K/2 \rceil$ approvals), we evaluate three alternatives: \emph{accuracy-weighted vote} ($w_j \propto \mathrm{acc}_j$; accept if $\sum_j w_j \cdot \mathbf{1}[J_j \text{ approves}] > 0.5$), \emph{threshold vote} (unanimity, $t{=}K$), and \emph{Bayesian calibration} ($P(\text{correct} \mid \mathbf{v}) \propto P(\text{correct}) \prod_j P(v_j \mid \text{correct})$; accept if posterior $> 0.5$).
47	
48	\subsection{Statistical Framework}
49	\label{sec:stat_framework}
50	
51	To disentangle the contributions of panel diversity and individual judge robustness, we fit a log-log OLS regression for each of the 12 conditions:
52	\begin{equation}
53	\label{eq:regression}
54	\log(\mathrm{ASR} + \epsilon) = \alpha + \beta_1 \log(K_{\mathrm{eff}}) + \beta_2 \log(\eta_{\max} + \epsilon),
55	\end{equation}
56	with $\epsilon = 10^{-6}$ to handle zero values (sensitivity to $\epsilon$ is analyzed in Appendix~\ref{app:epsilon}). Standardized coefficients $\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}} = \hat{\beta} \cdot \mathrm{SD}(\log x) / \mathrm{SD}(\log y)$ enable direct comparison of effect magnitudes across predictors and conditions. We apply Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) correction for multiple testing, controlling the false discovery rate (FDR) per predictor across the 12 conditions.
57	
58	Dominance analysis \citep{budescu1993dominance} decomposes $R^2$ into each predictor's average incremental contribution across all subsets. Generalized additive models (GAMs) with smooth terms test whether $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$'s effect direction varies between targeted attacks (prompt injection, score manipulation, position bias) and shared-bias attacks (sycophancy, verbosity, authority bias).
59	
60	\subsection{Robustness to Non-Independence}
61	\label{sec:robustness_method}
62	
63	The 455 panels share judges combinatorially (each appears in 91 panels), inducing correlated residuals. We apply three corrections \citep[see][for a practitioner's guide]{cameron2015practitioners}: clustered standard errors (SE; CR1, 3-position max-SE), mixed-effects models (random judge intercepts), and wild cluster bootstrap ($B{=}9{,}999$, Rademacher weights, 3-position max-$p$) following \citet{cameron2008bootstrap}. Cluster counts (13, ${\leq}11$, ${\leq}9$ at $K{=}3,5,7$) fall below the ${\geq}20$ threshold; we use Rademacher weights following \citet{mackinnon2018wild}. Fractional logit \citep{papke1996econometric} with clustered SE serves as an $\epsilon$-free robustness check (Appendix~\ref{app:robustness_methods}). Monte Carlo calibration (1{,}000 permutations, $B{=}999$) confirms the wild cluster bootstrap does not over-reject in any of 36 conditions (max null rejection rate ${=}1.6\%$ at nominal $\alpha{=}5\%$), establishing reported significance counts as conservative lower bounds (Appendix~\ref{app:monte_carlo}).
64	
```

The 2 benchmarks $\\times$ 6 attacks produce 12 conditions, each evaluated across all 455 panels (templates and implementation details in Appendix~\\ref{app:attacks}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Bench.} & \\textbf{Attack} & $\\boldsymbol{\\eta_{\\max}}$ & $\\boldsymbol{K_{\\mathrm{eff}}}$ & \\textbf{Dom.} \\\\\n\\midrule\nMMLU & Prompt inj.    & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nMMLU & Sycophancy     & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nMMLU & Score manip.   & n.s.    & $+^{*}$ & $K$ \\\\\nMMLU & Verbosity      & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nMMLU & Position bias  & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nMMLU & Authority bias & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nARC  & Prompt inj.    & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nARC  & Sycophancy     & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nARC  & Score manip.   & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nARC  & Verbosity      & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\nARC  & Position bias  & n.s.    & $+^{*}$ & $K$ \\\\\nARC  & Authority bias & $+^{*}$ & $+^{*}$ & Co \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Standardized regression coefficients for $\\eta_{\\max}$ and $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ across 12 conditions (455 panels, log-log OLS). $+^{*}$/$-^{*}$: FDR-significant positive/negative (Benjamini--Hochberg, $q < 0.05$); n.s.: not significant. Dom.\\ column: $\\eta$ = $\\eta_{\\max}$ sole dominant, $K$ = $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ sole dominant, Co = co-dominant \\citep{budescu1993dominance}.}\n\\label{tab:dominance}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Vulnerability Metrics}\n\nPer-judge vulnerability under attack $a$ is the fraction of items where judge $j$ is fooled: $\\eta_j(a) = \\Pr(X_j^a {=} 1)$, estimated empirically over the item set. The weakest-link vulnerability $\\eta_{\\max}(a) = \\max_{j \\in \\mathcal{P}} \\eta_j(a)$ captures the most susceptible member of each panel. The panel attack success rate $\\mathrm{ASR}(\\mathcal{P}, a)$ is the fraction of items where at least $t$ judges are simultaneously fooled. Panel diversity is quantified by $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ (Equation~\\ref{eq:keff}), computed from pairwise MI between judges' three-category winner labels (model~A wins, model~B wins, tie) on clean data, using the plug-in estimator with Miller--Madow bias correction. The $\\exp(-2\\,\\mathrm{MI}(i,j))$ term measures pairwise alienation: 1 when independent, 0 under full dependence. Under a Gaussian copula this yields $K_{\\mathrm{eff}} = K - \\frac{1}{K}\\sum_{i}\\sum_{j \\neq i} \\rho_{ij}^2$ \\citep{nelsen2006copulas}; goodness-of-fit rejects the Gaussian copula for 97--100\\% of pairs, yet plug-in MI and closed-form rankings agree closely ($\\rho{\\approx}0.86$; Appendix~\\ref{app:copula_gof}). Together, $\\eta_{\\max}$ (weakest-link channel) and $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ (diversity channel) serve as the two predictors in the regression framework below.\n\n\\subsection{Aggregation Methods}\n\nBeyond majority vote ($\\geq \\lceil K/2 \\rceil$ approvals), we evaluate three alternatives: \\emph{accuracy-weighted vote} ($w_j \\propto \\mathrm{acc}_j$; accept if $\\sum_j w_j \\cdot \\mathbf{1}[J_j \\text{ approves}] > 0.5$), \\emph{threshold vote} (unanimity, $t{=}K$), and \\emph{Bayesian calibration} ($P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\mathbf{v}) \\propto P(\\text{correct}) \\prod_j P(v_j \\mid \\text{correct})$; accept if posterior $> 0.5$).\n\n\\subsection{Statistical Framework}\n\\label{sec:stat_framework}\n\nTo disentangle the contributions of panel diversity and individual judge robustness, we fit a log-log OLS regression for each of the 12 conditions:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:regression}\n\\log(\\mathrm{ASR} + \\epsilon) = \\alpha + \\beta_1 \\log(K_{\\mathrm{eff}}) + \\beta_2 \\log(\\eta_{\\max} + \\epsilon),\n\\end{equation}\nwith $\\epsilon = 10^{-6}$ to handle zero values (sensitivity to $\\epsilon$ is analyzed in Appendix~\\ref{app:epsilon}). Standardized coefficients $\\hat{\\beta}_{\\mathrm{std}} = \\hat{\\beta} \\cdot \\mathrm{SD}(\\log x) / \\mathrm{SD}(\\log y)$ enable direct comparison of effect magnitudes across predictors and conditions. We apply Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) correction for multiple testing, controlling the false discovery rate (FDR) per predictor across the 12 conditions.\n\nDominance analysis \\citep{budescu1993dominance} decomposes $R^2$ into each predictor's average incremental contribution across all subsets. Generalized additive models (GAMs) with smooth terms test whether $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$'s effect direction varies between targeted attacks (prompt injection, score manipulation, position bias) and shared-bias attacks (sycophancy, verbosity, authority bias).\n\n\\subsection{Robustness to Non-Independence}\n\\label{sec:robustness_method}\n\nThe 455 panels share judges combinatorially (each appears in 91 panels), inducing correlated residuals. We apply three corrections \\citep[see][for a practitioner's guide]{cameron2015practitioners}: clustered standard errors (SE; CR1, 3-position max-SE), mixed-effects models (random judge intercepts), and wild cluster bootstrap ($B{=}9{,}999$, Rademacher weights, 3-position max-$p$) following \\citet{cameron2008bootstrap}. Cluster counts (13, ${\\leq}11$, ${\\leq}9$ at $K{=}3,5,7$) fall below the ${\\geq}20$ threshold; we use Rademacher weights following \\citet{mackinnon2018wild}. Fractional logit \\citep{papke1996econometric} with clustered SE serves as an $\\epsilon$-free robustness check (Appendix~\\ref{app:robustness_methods}). Monte Carlo calibration (1{,}000 permutations, $B{=}999$) confirms the wild cluster bootstrap does not over-reject in any of 36 conditions (max null rejection rate ${=}1.6\\%$ at nominal $\\alpha{=}5\\%$), establishing reported significance counts as conservative lower bounds (Appendix~\\ref{app:monte_carlo}).\n",
      "numLines": 64,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 64
    }

```text
1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\subsection{Weakest-Link Dominance at \texorpdfstring{$K{=}3$}{K=3}}
5	\label{sec:dominance}
6	
7	Table~\ref{tab:dominance} reports the log-log OLS regression (Equation~\ref{eq:regression}) for all 12 conditions across the 455 panels ($K{=}3$, 15 judges from Table~\ref{tab:models}).
8	
9	Under OLS, $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is positive and FDR-significant in all 12/12 conditions. $\eta_{\max}$ is significant in 10/12 (exceptions: MMLU $\times$ score manipulation and ARC $\times$ position bias), consistent with Proposition~\ref{thm:monotonicity}. Dominance analysis yields 10 co-dominant, 2 $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$-dominant, and zero $\eta_{\max}$-dominant conditions. However, the $r = 0.70$ $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$--$\eta_{\max}$ correlation raises confound concerns, and OLS ignores the non-independence of the 455 panels (Appendix~\ref{app:keff_dist}).
10	
11	Each judge appears in $\binom{14}{2} = 91$ of the 455 panels, and each pair co-occurs in 13. This shared structure causes OLS to underestimate standard errors by approximately $2.5{\times}$. Figure~\ref{fig:robustness} compares OLS against three corrections: both predictors attenuate under bootstrap ($\eta_{\max}$ 3/12, $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ 4/12), with clustered SE widest ($K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ 0/12; Appendix~\ref{app:forest_plot}). A crossed random-effects analysis accounting for the multi-membership structure of shared judges confirms $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance at $K{\geq}5$ under this more conservative correction (Appendix~\ref{app:crossed_re}).
12	
13	\begin{table}[t]
14	\centering
15	\small
16	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
17	\toprule
18	\textbf{Method} & $\boldsymbol{\eta_{\max}}$ \textbf{sig} & $\boldsymbol{K_{\mathrm{eff}}}$ \textbf{sig} \\
19	\midrule
20	OLS                    & 10/12 & 12/12 \\
21	Clustered SE           & 4/12  & 0/12  \\
22	Mixed effects          & 7/12  & 3/12  \\
23	Wild bootstrap         & 3/12  & 4/12  \\
24	Frac.\ logit (clust.)  & 7/12  & 9/12  \\
25	\bottomrule
26	\end{tabular}
27	\caption{Inference method sensitivity at $K{=}3$ (455 panels, 12 conditions). $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance ranges from 0/12 (clustered SE) to 12/12 (OLS); all methods preserve positive direction (12/12).}
28	\label{tab:method_comparison}
29	\end{table}
30	
31	\begin{figure}[t]
32	\centering
33	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_robustness_comparison.pdf}
34	\caption{Significance counts (FDR $< 0.05$) for $\eta_{\max}$ and $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ across four correction methods over 12 experimental conditions ($K{=}3$). Wild cluster bootstrap and clustered standard errors are recommended for the 15-judge combinatorial design.}
35	\label{fig:robustness}
36	\end{figure}
37	
38	Power analysis yields a minimum detectable effect (MDE) of $\beta_{\mathrm{std}} = 0.14$ at 80\% power (decreasing to 0.048 at $K{=}5$; Appendix~\ref{app:power_analysis}). The bootstrap attenuation at $K{=}3$ (OLS 12/12 $\to$ bootstrap 4/12) is consistent with this MDE, though positive coefficient direction (12/12) is preserved across all methods (Appendix~\ref{app:random_subsample}). Monte Carlo calibration (1{,}000 permutations per condition) confirms the wild cluster bootstrap is conservative: the maximum null rejection rate is $1.6\%$ at nominal $\alpha{=}5\%$ across all 36 conditions ($12 \times 3$ panel sizes), so reported significance counts are lower bounds (Appendix~\ref{app:monte_carlo}).
39	
40	\subsection{The Diminishing Weakest Link Across Panel Sizes}
41	\label{sec:cross_k}
42	
43	Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison} reveals that the $K{=}3$ pattern is method-sensitive: $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance ranges from 0/12 (clustered SE) to 12/12 (OLS), with fractional logit giving 9/12. This contrasts with $K{\geq}5$, where $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$'s dominance is consistent across methods (bootstrap 7/12 at $K{=}5$; fractional logit 9/12).
44	
45	We repeat the wild cluster bootstrap at $K \in \{3, 5, 7\}$, constructing all $\binom{15}{K}$ panels with $K$-position max-$p$ correction.
46	
47	\begin{figure*}[t]
48	\centering
49	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.48\textwidth}
50	\centering
51	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig_regime_transition.pdf}
52	\caption{Predictor shift across panel sizes. $\eta_{\max}$ significance (orange) declines from 3/12 to 2/12, while $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (blue) rises from 4/12 to 7/12. Shaded region: $K^* \in (3, 5)$. Bootstrap: $B{=}9999$, Rademacher, max-$p$, BH FDR $\alpha{=}0.05$.}
53	\label{fig:regime_transition}
54	\end{minipage}\hfill
55	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.48\textwidth}
56	\centering
57	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig5_forest_plot.pdf}
58	\caption{Per-condition standardized $\hat{\beta}$ with 95\% CI for $\eta_{\max}$ (left) and $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (right) at $K{=}3$ (wild cluster bootstrap).}
59	\label{fig:forest_plot}
60	\end{minipage}
61	\end{figure*}
62	
63	Meta-analytic pooling (DerSimonian--Laird random effects across 12 conditions) quantifies the trajectories independent of significance thresholds. $\eta_{\max}$'s pooled $\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{std}}$ declines monotonically: $0.264$ [$0.158, 0.369$] at $K{=}3$ ($I^2{=}91.3\%$), $0.102$ [$-0.055, 0.259$] at $K{=}5$ ($I^2{=}99.4\%$), $0.084$ [$-0.083, 0.251$] at $K{=}7$ ($I^2{=}99.8\%$); CI crosses zero at $K{\geq}5$. $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$'s pooled coefficient remains positive: $0.356$ [$0.267, 0.445$] ($I^2{=}87.7\%$), $0.436$ [$0.318, 0.553$] ($I^2{=}98.9\%$), $0.401$ [$0.298, 0.503$] ($I^2{=}99.4\%$)---heterogeneous magnitudes, consistent direction. The high $I^2$ values indicate substantial between-condition heterogeneity; pooled estimates summarize the central trend but individual conditions vary widely. Figure~\ref{fig:regime_transition} shows significance counts and Figure~\ref{fig:forest_plot} displays per-condition effect sizes with confidence intervals (per-condition dominance in Figure~\ref{fig:dominance_heatmap}, Appendix~\ref{app:extended_analysis}). The $\eta_{\max}$ decline holds under both $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\kappa$-diversity specifications. Restricting to the four $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$-stable attacks ($\rho{\geq}0.91$; Appendix~\ref{app:attacked_keff}) confirms the signal does not depend on measurement-unstable conditions.
64	
65	
66	\subsection{Random vs.\ Adaptive Fault Contrast}
67	\label{sec:rf_contrast}
68	
69	We simulate non-adversarial noise by randomly flipping judge votes at observed per-judge error rates. Random faults yield $R^2 = 0.730$ [$0.718, 0.745$] ($K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significant 2/2); adaptive attack reduces predictability ($R^2{=}0.285$ [$0.270, 0.301$] at $K{=}5$) but $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ retains power (7/12). The predictability gap is $\Delta{=}0.446$ [$0.430, 0.460$]. The positive $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ coefficient under adaptive attack contrasts with the Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785essai}, where independence among voters is protective. Under adversarial manipulation, independent judges present distinct attack surfaces rather than redundant defenses; this reversal is consistent with adversarial ensemble theory \citep{goodfellow2015adversarial,kariyappa2019improving}.
70	
71	\subsection{Positive Direction Across Attack Types}
72	\label{sec:gam_taxonomy}
73	
74	GAM bootstrap confirms positive $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ direction in all 12 conditions (88.9--100\% positive rate; Figure~\ref{fig:gam_split}), with no magnitude difference between targeted and shared-bias attacks (Kruskal--Wallis $p{=}0.200$, $0.423$).
75	
76	\subsection{Aggregation Rules and the Diversity Channel}
77	\label{sec:aggregation}
78	
79	Table~\ref{tab:aggregation} reports regression results for four aggregation methods across all 12 conditions.
80	
81	\begin{table}[t]
82	\centering
83	\small
84	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
85	\toprule
86	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{$\eta_{\max}$ sig} & \textbf{$K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ sig} & \textbf{$K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ $\beta$ dir.} & \textbf{$R^2$} \\
87	\midrule
88	Threshold       & 12/12 & 11/12 & 11+/0$-$  & 0.829 \\
89	Majority        & 10/12 & 12/12 & 12+/0$-$  & 0.327 \\
90	Weighted        & 9/12  & 12/12 & 12+/0$-$  & 0.328 \\
91	Bayesian        & 9/12  & 6/12  & 5+/1$-$   & 0.087 \\
92	\bottomrule
93	\end{tabular}
94	\caption{Aggregation method comparison ($K{=}3$, 15 models, 455 panels, OLS log-log). Significance counts are FDR-corrected across 12 conditions. $R^2$ is the mean across conditions. Table~\ref{tab:dominance} uses per-condition FDR; this table uses pooled FDR across conditions.}
95	\label{tab:aggregation}
96	\end{table}
97	
98	
99	Predictability ranges from threshold ($R^2{=}0.829$) to Bayesian ($R^2{=}0.087$). The Bayesian $R^2$ near zero implies that panel composition is almost unpredictive under calibrated aggregation---the diversity and weakest-link predictors may only be relevant under specific aggregation rules rather than being intrinsic panel properties. Bayesian calibration attenuates $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (5+/1$-$) but $\eta_{\max}$ persists (9/12), yielding partial defense (Appendix~\ref{app:aggregation_details}). At $K{=}5$, all four methods converge to identical $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance (7/12; Appendix~\ref{app:aggregation_k5}); at $K{=}7$, methods diverge again, with Bayesian neutralization returning while threshold voting strengthens (Appendix~\ref{app:aggregation_k7}).
100	
101	\paragraph{Fractional Logit Robustness.}
102	Fractional logit \citep{papke1996econometric} with clustered SE shows consistent positive association: $K_{\mathrm{eff}}$ significance is 9/12 across all panel sizes ($K{=}3,5,7$), with 98.6\% directional concordance with OLS (71/72; Appendix~\ref{app:robustness_checks}).
103

Bootstrap: $B{=}9999$, Rademacher, max-$p$, BH FDR $\\alpha{=}0.05$.}\n\\label{fig:regime_transition}\n\\end{minipage}\\hfill\n\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.48\\textwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig5_forest_plot.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-condition standardized $\\hat{\\beta}$ with 95\\% CI for $\\eta_{\\max}$ (left) and $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ (right) at $K{=}3$ (wild cluster bootstrap).}\n\\label{fig:forest_plot}\n\\end{minipage}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nMeta-analytic pooling (DerSimonian--Laird random effects across 12 conditions) quantifies the trajectories independent of significance thresholds. $\\eta_{\\max}$'s pooled $\\hat{\\beta}_{\\mathrm{std}}$ declines monotonically: $0.264$ [$0.158, 0.369$] at $K{=}3$ ($I^2{=}91.3\\%$), $0.102$ [$-0.055, 0.259$] at $K{=}5$ ($I^2{=}99.4\\%$), $0.084$ [$-0.083, 0.251$] at $K{=}7$ ($I^2{=}99.8\\%$); CI crosses zero at $K{\\geq}5$. $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$'s pooled coefficient remains positive: $0.356$ [$0.267, 0.445$] ($I^2{=}87.7\\%$), $0.436$ [$0.318, 0.553$] ($I^2{=}98.9\\%$), $0.401$ [$0.298, 0.503$] ($I^2{=}99.4\\%$)---heterogeneous magnitudes, consistent direction. The high $I^2$ values indicate substantial between-condition heterogeneity; pooled estimates summarize the central trend but individual conditions vary widely. Figure~\\ref{fig:regime_transition} shows significance counts and Figure~\\ref{fig:forest_plot} displays per-condition effect sizes with confidence intervals (per-condition dominance in Figure~\\ref{fig:dominance_heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_analysis}). The $\\eta_{\\max}$ decline holds under both $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\\kappa$-diversity specifications. Restricting to the four $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$-stable attacks ($\\rho{\\geq}0.91$; Appendix~\\ref{app:attacked_keff}) confirms the signal does not depend on measurement-unstable conditions.\n\n\n\\subsection{Random vs.\\ Adaptive Fault Contrast}\n\\label{sec:rf_contrast}\n\nWe simulate non-adversarial noise by randomly flipping judge votes at observed per-judge error rates. Random faults yield $R^2 = 0.730$ [$0.718, 0.745$] ($K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ significant 2/2); adaptive attack reduces predictability ($R^2{=}0.285$ [$0.270, 0.301$] at $K{=}5$) but $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ retains power (7/12). The predictability gap is $\\Delta{=}0.446$ [$0.430, 0.460$]. The positive $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ coefficient under adaptive attack contrasts with the Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785essai}, where independence among voters is protective. Under adversarial manipulation, independent judges present distinct attack surfaces rather than redundant defenses; this reversal is consistent with adversarial ensemble theory \\citep{goodfellow2015adversarial,kariyappa2019improving}.\n\n\\subsection{Positive Direction Across Attack Types}\n\\label{sec:gam_taxonomy}\n\nGAM bootstrap confirms positive $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ direction in all 12 conditions (88.9--100\\% positive rate; Figure~\\ref{fig:gam_split}), with no magnitude difference between targeted and shared-bias attacks (Kruskal--Wallis $p{=}0.200$, $0.423$).\n\n\\subsection{Aggregation Rules and the Diversity Channel}\n\\label{sec:aggregation}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:aggregation} reports regression results for four aggregation methods across all 12 conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Method} & \\textbf{$\\eta_{\\max}$ sig} & \\textbf{$K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ sig} & \\textbf{$K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ $\\beta$ dir.} & \\textbf{$R^2$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nThreshold       & 12/12 & 11/12 & 11+/0$-$  & 0.829 \\\\\nMajority        & 10/12 & 12/12 & 12+/0$-$  & 0.327 \\\\\nWeighted        & 9/12  & 12/12 & 12+/0$-$  & 0.328 \\\\\nBayesian        & 9/12  & 6/12  & 5+/1$-$   & 0.087 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Aggregation method comparison ($K{=}3$, 15 models, 455 panels, OLS log-log). Significance counts are FDR-corrected across 12 conditions. $R^2$ is the mean across conditions. Table~\\ref{tab:dominance} uses per-condition FDR; this table uses pooled FDR across conditions.}\n\\label{tab:aggregation}\n\\end{table}\n\n\nPredictability ranges from threshold ($R^2{=}0.829$) to Bayesian ($R^2{=}0.087$). The Bayesian $R^2$ near zero implies that panel composition is almost unpredictive under calibrated aggregation---the diversity and weakest-link predictors may only be relevant under specific aggregation rules rather than being intrinsic panel properties. Bayesian calibration attenuates $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ (5+/1$-$) but $\\eta_{\\max}$ persists (9/12), yielding partial defense (Appendix~\\ref{app:aggregation_details}). At $K{=}5$, all four methods converge to identical $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ significance (7/12; Appendix~\\ref{app:aggregation_k5}); at $K{=}7$, methods diverge again, with Bayesian neutralization returning while threshold voting strengthens (Appendix~\\ref{app:aggregation_k7}).\n\n\\paragraph{Fractional Logit Robustness.}\nFractional logit \\citep{papke1996econometric} with clustered SE shows consistent positive association: $K_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ significance is 9/12 across all panel sizes ($K{=}3,5,7$), with 98.6\\% directional concordance with OLS (71/72; Appendix~\\ref{app:robustness_checks}).\n",
